</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->19<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The increased exposure of Marvel characters in movies and television shows can create revenue
opportunities for us through increased sales of licensed merchandise. Producing films ourselves
provides us with more control of our film projects, gives us greater flexibility to coordinate the
timing of licensing programs around Marvel-branded theatrical releases and provides us with the
opportunity for a meaningful source of profits. The operations of developing and producing our own
theatrical releases are reported in our Film Production segment, the funding for which comes
primarily from our $525&nbsp;million film facility. We intend to self-produce all future films based on
our characters that have not already been licensed to third parties.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our operating results were stronger in 2008 than in 2007, due largely to the 2008 release of
our first two self-produced films, <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>. As discussed below, we will
not release any self-produced films in 2009 and our only licensed film scheduled for release in
2009 is <I>X-Men Origins: Wolverine</I>. As a result, we expect our operating results to be lower in 2009
than in 2008 or 2007, when <I>Spider-Man 3 </I>was released. Our next self-produced films, <I>Iron Man 2 </I>and
<I>Thor</I>, are scheduled for release in 2010.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Licensing</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our Licensing segment is responsible for the licensing, promotion and brand management for all
of our characters worldwide. We pursue a strategy, where feasible, of concentrating our licensee
relationships with fewer, larger licensees who demonstrate the financial and merchandising
capability to manage our portfolio of both classic and movie properties. A key focus is
negotiating strong minimum guarantees while keeping royalty rates competitive.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Another strategy of the Licensing segment&#146;s consumer products program is to create new revenue
opportunities by further segmenting our properties to appeal to new demographic profiles.
Initiatives such as Marvel Super Hero Squad, Marvel Extreme, Marvel Heroes and Marvel Comics (the
retro depiction of our characters) have all helped the licensing business expand beyond its
traditional classic and event-driven properties.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Major entertainment events play an important role in driving sales of our licensed products.
In 2007, our Licensing segment revenue reflected the benefit of the May&nbsp;2007 release of the movie
<I>Spider-Man 3. </I>The Licensing segment&#146;s 2007 initiatives were focused on merchandising our
self-produced movies: <I>Iron Man</I>, which was released on May&nbsp;2, 2008, and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>, which
was released on June&nbsp;13, 2008. Our 2008 Licensing segment revenue benefited from the release of
those movies, but not as significantly as 2007 Licensing segment revenue benefited from the release
of <I>Spider-Man 3</I>. We expect that our 2009 Licensing segment revenue will be lower than in 2008 as
there will only be one major entertainment event in 2009, <I>X-Men Origins: Wolverine</I>, expected to be
domestically released on May&nbsp;1, 2009 by Twentieth Century Fox. In addition, although we have many
licensees that are large companies, the majority of our consumer product licensees are small to
medium sized companies, located throughout the world, that rely on access to credit to produce and
distribute their products. As a result of world-wide tightening of credit markets, some of our
licensees may have difficulties producing and distributing goods. In addition, due to the
lessening of consumer demand resulting from the global recessionary environment, some of our
licensees may have difficulties obtaining sufficient sales orders. These macro-economic factors
(see &#147;Item&nbsp;1A &#151; Risk Factors&#148;) are another reason for our expectation that 2009 Licensing segment
revenue will be lower than in 2008. We believe the negative impact of these macro-economic factors
on the Licensing segment may be partially mitigated as a result of the non-durable nature of the
products sold by most of our licensees and the low prices at which the majority of these products
are sold.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We typically enter into multi-year merchandise license agreements that specify minimum royalty
payments and include a significant down payment upon signing. We recognize license revenue when the
earnings process is complete, including, for instance, the determination that the credit-worthiness
of the licensee reasonably assures collectibility of any outstanding minimum royalty payments. If
the earnings process is complete with respect to all required minimum royalty payments, then we
record as revenue the present value of those payments.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->20<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The earnings process is not complete if, among other things, we have significant continuing
involvement under the license, we have placed restrictions on the licensee&#146;s ability to exploit the
rights conveyed under the contract or we owe a performance obligation to the licensee. In the case
where we have significant continuing involvement or where any restrictions remain on the licensee&#146;s
rights (e.g., no sales of products based on a specific character allowed until a future date), we
recognize revenue as the licensee reports its sales and corresponding royalty obligation to us.
Where we have a performance obligation, minimum royalty collections are not recognized until our
performance obligation has been satisfied. Minimum payments collected in advance of recognition are
recorded as deferred revenue. In any case where we are unable to determine that the licensee is
sufficiently creditworthy, we recognize revenue only to the extent of cash collections. When
cumulative reported royalties exceed the minimum royalty payments, the excess royalties are
recorded as revenue when collected and are referred to as &#147;overages&#148;.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">As discussed above, beginning in 2008 we are including revenues earned from Hasbro, and
related expenses, in our Licensing segment.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Publishing</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The Publishing segment is focused on strengthening its Super Hero graphic fiction presence in
its primary distribution channels such as the direct and mass market, and expanding its reach to a
broader demographic by providing all ages and new reader products in the book market and online.
In 2008, Marvel launched a major comic book crossover series, <I>Secret Invasion</I>, which involves many
of the Marvel characters and features tie-ins to many other Marvel publications, similar to the
<I>Civil War </I>series that was a top-selling comic book in 2007. <I>Secret Invasion </I>ran from April through
December&nbsp;2008. The third volume of the <I>Dark Tower </I>series and the first volume of <I>The Stand </I>series
were released in October&nbsp;2008. The momentum of these efforts will take us into and through <I>the
Dark Reign </I>and <I>Ultimatum </I>publishing events that will occur in the first half of 2009, which will be
supplemented with a variety of Wolverine product to be distributed around the <I>X-Men Origins:
Wolverine </I>movie expected to be domestically released on May&nbsp;1, 2009 by Twentieth Century Fox. We
will also see the first collections for <I>The Stand </I>and <I>the Ender&#146;s Game </I>series along with the
continued publication of the <I>Dark Tower</I>. However, due to the macro-economic factors discussed
above, we believe that Publishing segment revenue in 2009 will be lower than in 2008 as consumer
spending will be down, retailers look to reduce inventory and advertising budgets remain
constrained. The current economic climate may also lead to a reduction in the number of large book
retailers and direct-market retailers. Direct-market retailers are generally small business
entities, many of which rely on access to credit that may be more difficult to obtain.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The Publishing segment has continued its development and investment in digital media,
resulting in increased content on our Marvel Digital Comics Unlimited service, where we currently
have over 5,000 previously published Marvel comic books available for viewing online in a
proprietary viewer. We have also added more content to our websites, including videos, casual
games, news and character biographies. In early 2008, we launched a separate website,
www.marvelkids.com, featuring Marvel characters and content developed for children ages 6-11.
During 2008, we also expanded our distribution of digital media content through arrangements with
third-party websites such as YouTube and iTunes. We expect continued growth and diversification in
Marvel Online revenues as we continue to increase our online presence. However, our expectations
for online revenue growth, expected in large part to be achieved through increased advertising
revenues, have been reduced because of the macro-economic factors discussed above, which have had a
negative impact on industry-wide online advertising. For the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007
and 2006, the revenue of our digital media group was $2.5&nbsp;million, $1.0&nbsp;million and $0.3&nbsp;million,
respectively, and the expenses of our digital media group were $4.5&nbsp;million, $1.6&nbsp;million and $0.1
million, respectively.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Film Production</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In 2008, we released our first two self-produced films: <I>Iron Man </I>on May 2 and <I>The Incredible
Hulk </I>on June&nbsp;13. We are currently developing four films for release in 2010 and 2011: <I>Iron Man 2</I>,
<I>Thor</I>, <I>The First Avenger: Captain America </I>and <I>The Avengers</I>. The scheduled release dates of those
films are, respectively, May&nbsp;7, 2010, July&nbsp;16, 2010, May&nbsp;6, 2011 and July&nbsp;15, 2011. After the
release of each film, we begin to recognize revenue and to amortize our film inventory as described
below.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->21<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Film Inventory</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In general, we are responsible for all of the costs of developing and producing our feature
films. The film&#146;s distributor is responsible for the out-of-pocket costs, charges and expenses
(including contingent compensation and residual costs, to a defined limit) incurred in the
distribution, manufacturing, printing and advertising, marketing, publicizing and promotion of the
film in all media (referred to in the aggregate as the distributor&#146;s costs). The distributor&#146;s
costs are not included in film inventory.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We account for our film inventory under the guidance provided by AICPA Statement of Position
00-2, &#147;Accounting by Producers or Distributors of Films&#148; (&#147;SOP 00-2&#148;). We capitalize all direct
film production costs, such as labor costs, visual effects and set construction. Those capitalized
costs, along with capitalized production overhead and capitalized interest costs, appear on our
balance sheet as an asset called film inventory. Production overhead includes allocable costs,
including cash and stock compensation and benefits, of individuals or departments with exclusive or
significant responsibility for the production of films. Capitalization of production overhead and
interest costs commences upon completion of the requirements for funding the production under the
film facility and ceases upon completion of the production.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We also capitalize the costs of projects in development into film inventory. Those costs
consist primarily of script development. In the event that a film does not begin pre-production
within three years from the time of the first capitalized transaction, or if an earlier decision is
made to abandon the project, all capitalized costs related to these projects are expensed. During
2008 and 2007, $1.7 and $1.3&nbsp;million, respectively, of film development costs were written off and
included in selling, general and administrative expenses in the accompanying consolidated
statements of net income.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Once a film is released, using the individual-film-forecast computation method, the amount of
film inventory relating to that film is amortized and included in each period&#146;s costs of revenue in
the proportion that the film&#146;s revenue during the period bears to the film&#146;s then-estimated total
revenue, net of the distributor&#146;s costs, over a period not to exceed ten years (ultimate revenues).
Estimates of ultimate revenues for each film are regularly reviewed and revised as necessary based
on the latest available information. Reductions in those revenue estimates could result in the
write-off, or the acceleration of the amortization, of film inventory in that reporting period;
increases in those revenue estimates could result in reduced amortization in that period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, our Film Production segment had film inventory of $181.6&nbsp;million,
primarily for the <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>productions.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Film Revenue</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The amount of revenue recognized from our films in any given period depends on the timing,
accuracy and sufficiency of the information we receive from our distributors.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">After remitting to us five percent of the film&#146;s gross receipts, the distributor is entitled
to retain a fee based upon the film&#146;s gross receipts and to recoup all of its costs on a
film-by-film basis prior to our receiving any additional share of film receipts. Any of the
distributor&#146;s costs for a film that are not recouped against receipts for that film are borne by
the distributor. Our share of the film&#146;s receipts, as described above, is recognized as revenue
when reported due to us by the distributor. We received minimum guarantees from local distributors
in five territories in connection with the release of <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>. In those
territories, we began to recognize revenue when the film was made available for exhibition in
theaters.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Revenue from the sale of home video units is recognized when our distributors report as due to
us the home video sale proceeds that they have collected from retailers. We provide for future
mark-downs and returns of home entertainment product at the time the related revenue is recognized,
using estimates. Our estimates are calculated by analyzing a combination of our distributors&#146;
historical returns and mark-down practices, our distributors&#146; estimates of returns of our home
video units, current economic trends, projections of consumer demand for our home video units and
point-of-sale data available from retailers. We periodically review our estimates using the latest
information available.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->22<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Revenue from both free and pay television licensing agreements is recognized at the time the
production is made available for exhibition in those markets.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 1%"><I>Film Facility</I>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The film facility enables us to independently finance the development and production costs of
up to ten feature films, including films that may feature the following Marvel characters, whose
theatrical film rights are pledged as collateral to secure the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Also included as collateral for the film facility are the theatrical film rights to many of
the supporting characters that would be most closely associated with the featured characters and
character families. For example, the theatrical film rights to The Incredible Hulk&#146;s girlfriend,
Betty Ross, and his nemesis, Abomination, are both pledged as collateral to the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We are currently developing a movie based on the character Thor and expect to obtain the
consent of the film facility lenders to finance and produce that film through the film facility, in
which case we will pledge the theatrical film rights to Thor and various related characters as
additional collateral to secure the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">While theatrical films featuring the characters listed above may be financed and produced by
us only through the film facility, we retain all other rights associated with those characters. In
addition, we may continue to license our other characters for movie productions by third parties,
obtain financing to produce movies based on those other characters ourselves or with others or,
with the consent of the film facility lenders, finance and produce films based on those other
characters through the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We fund, from working capital and other sources, the incremental overhead expenses and costs
of developing each film to the stage at which the conditions for an initial borrowing for the film
are met under the film facility. If the film&#146;s initial funding conditions are met under the film
facility, we are able to borrow up to 67% of our budgeted production costs including an amount
equal to our incremental overhead expenses related to that film, but not exceeding 2% of the film&#146;s
budget. If the initial funding conditions are not met, we will be unable to borrow these amounts
under the film facility. Beginning with our third film, upon meeting the film&#146;s initial funding
conditions, we will be responsible to immediately fund 33% of that film&#146;s budget using our own
funds.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We recorded interest expense related to the film facility, net of interest capitalized, of
$19.0&nbsp;million, $13.7&nbsp;million and $12.8&nbsp;million during the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007 and
2006, respectively. Interest charges associated with borrowings to fund the productions were
capitalized, rather than expensed, until the completion of production. The productions of <I>Iron Man</I>
and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>were completed in the second quarter of 2008. Starting in the second
quarter of 2008, therefore, our interest expense began to increase significantly, as we began to
expense, rather than capitalize, interest on the amounts borrowed to fund the <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The
Incredible Hulk </I>productions. In 2008, 2007 and 2006, we capitalized interest costs of $5.1
million, $8.4&nbsp;million and $0.2&nbsp;million, respectively and those amounts were included in film
inventory.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our consolidated net sales of $676.2&nbsp;million for 2008 were $190.4&nbsp;million higher than net
sales in 2007. The increase primarily reflects the $254.6&nbsp;million increase in Film Production net
sales related to the theatrical releases during 2008 of <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>. This
increase was partially offset by a 15% decline in licensing net sales and, within All Other, a
decline in sales due to our exit from toy manufacturing operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Licensing segment net sales decreased $50.8&nbsp;million during 2008, reflecting a $64.6&nbsp;million
decrease in Joint Venture revenue (to $57.4&nbsp;million, primarily overages) related to the May&nbsp;2007
release of Spider-Man 3 and a $14.8&nbsp;million decrease in licensing audit claim settlements,
resulting from an unusually high amount of settlement revenue in 2007. These decreases were
partially offset by increases of $13.0&nbsp;million and $11.1&nbsp;million, respectively, in domestic and
foreign licensing revenue, excluding the Joint Venture. The increases in domestic and foreign
licensing were primarily due to amounts that were previously recorded as deferred revenue until the
second quarter of 2008, when most licensees were first permitted to begin selling merchandise
relating to <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>, partially offset by a decrease in overages that
primarily resulted from increased licensing contract renewal activity. The increase in Film
Production revenue and the significant decrease in Joint Venture revenue caused 2008 Licensing
segment net sales to decrease as a percentage of consolidated net sales from 71% in 2007 to 43% in
2008.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->24<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net sales from the Publishing segment were consistent from 2007 to 2008 with a decrease of
$0.3&nbsp;million to $125.4&nbsp;million in 2008. This decrease reflects a $2.2&nbsp;million reduction in our
custom comic book sales due to a decrease in the scale and number of projects, a $1.3&nbsp;million
reduction in advertising revenue, resulting from decreased spending of advertising dollars by
larger corporate advertisers and a $0.6&nbsp;million reduction in sales of trade paperbacks. These
reductions were offset by an increase of $0.8&nbsp;million in our online comic subscription revenue and
a $0.7&nbsp;million increase in our online advertisement sales, both due to the growth of our digital
media business in 2008, and by a $2.4&nbsp;million increase in our comic book sales related to
limited-edition comic book series events. Comic book sales in 2008 benefited from strong unit
sales of <I>Secret Invasion</I>, a limited-edition comic book series. Comic book sales in 2007 benefited
from strong unit sales of the final two comic-book issues of the <I>Civil War </I>series; <I>The Death of
Captain America; the Stephen King </I>series, <I>Dark Tower: The Gunslinger Born; </I>and the <I>World War Hulk</I>
series. Publishing segment net sales decreased as a percentage of consolidated net sales from 26%
in 2007 to 19% in 2008, primarily because of film production revenues in 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net sales from the Film Production segment were $254.6&nbsp;million in 2008, related to the
theatrical releases of <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk. </I>There were no Film Production segment
revenues in 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net sales included in All Other represent our former toy manufacturing operations, which we
exited in 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated cost of revenues increased $130.6&nbsp;million to $191.5&nbsp;million during 2008 compared
with 2007, primarily reflecting the amortization of film inventory in our Film Production segment.
This increase was partially offset by the reduction of toy-production costs resulting from our exit
from the toy business. Consequently, our consolidated cost of revenues as a percentage of sales
increased to 28% during 2008, as compared to 13% in 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Publishing segment cost of revenues for comic book and trade paperback publishing consists of
art, editorial, and printing costs. The $3.0&nbsp;million increase in Publishing segment cost of
revenues is primarily associated with the increase in comic titles published (1,128 in 2008 vs. 974
in 2007). Publishing segment cost of revenues as a percentage of Publishing segment net sales
increased from 42% in 2007 to 44% in 2008, primarily reflecting the impact of rising costs of
talent combined with a reduction in the size of print runs and, to a lesser extent, an increase in
paper costs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Film Production segment cost of revenues during the 2008 period consisted of the amortization
of film inventory as revenue was generated from the <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>feature films.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Cost of revenues included in All Other primarily consists of our former toy production
activities.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated selling, general and administrative (&#147;SG&#038;A&#148;) expenses of $138.5&nbsp;million in 2008
were $8.6&nbsp;million lower than SG&#038;A expenses in 2007, primarily reflecting decreases in SG&#038;A expenses
in the Licensing segment, which were partially offset by increases in SG&#038;A expenses in the Film
Production and Publishing segments. Consolidated SG&#038;A as a percentage of net sales during 2008
decreased to 20%, from 30% in 2007, primarily reflecting increased revenue from the Film Production
segment and the impact of items in the Licensing segment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Licensing segment SG&#038;A expenses consist primarily of payroll, agents&#146; foreign-sales
commissions and royalties owed to movie studios and talent for their share of license royalty
income, which are variable expenses based on licensing revenues. We pay movie studio licensees up
to 50% of merchandising-based royalty revenue (after certain contractually agreed-upon deductions)
from the licensing of both &#147;classic&#148; and &#147;movie&#148; versions of characters featured in the films.
Licensing segment SG&#038;A expenses of $70.9&nbsp;million for 2008 were $16.4&nbsp;million less than in 2007.
The decrease was primarily because of changes in information that we received from Sony Pictures.
In particular, on the basis of information that Sony Pictures provided late in 2007, we accrued an
expense of $11.5&nbsp;million in that year for royalties payable to actors starring in the Spider-Man
movies for the use of their likenesses in licensed products. That accrual increased our 2007
Licensing segment SG&#038;A. Sony Pictures then retracted that information and replaced it with new
information in 2008, and on the basis of the new information we reduced the accrual by $8.3
million. That reduction decreased our 2008 Licensing segment SG&#038;A. The accrual and the reduction
together produced a $19.8&nbsp;million downward effect on our year-over-year change in Licensing segment
SG&#038;A. Another downward effect was created by a $4.7&nbsp;million non-recurring charge recorded in 2007
related to a contractual obligation. These decreases were partially offset by increases of $2.6
million in foreign sales commissions attributable to higher commission-based foreign revenues, $2.6
million in royalties primarily owed to movie studios and $2.5&nbsp;million in promotions and other
selling expenses. The increase in royalties is primarily due to the change in sales-mix resulting
in an increase in non-Joint Venture net sales and a decrease in Joint Venture Licensing segment net
sales, of which Sony Pictures&#146; share of royalty income is reflected as minority interest expense
rather than SG&#038;A, whereas other studios&#146; shares of license royalty income is recorded within SG&#038;A
expense. As a percentage of Licensing segment net sales, Licensing segment SG&#038;A was consistent at
24% during 2008 compared with 25% in 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Publishing segment SG&#038;A expenses consist primarily of payroll, distribution fees and other
miscellaneous overhead costs. Publishing segment SG&#038;A expenses increased $2.9&nbsp;million during 2008
over 2007, principally due to increased employee compensation of $1.9&nbsp;million as a result of
increased headcount to service the growth of our digital media business. In addition, this
increase also reflects an increase of $0.6&nbsp;million in the consulting and hosting fees related to
the digital media web infrastructure.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->26<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">SG&#038;A expenses for our Film Production segment consist primarily of employee compensation and
the allocated expenses associated with our California office. The costs of marketing and promoting
our films are borne by our distributors. Film Production SG&#038;A expenses increased $7.0&nbsp;million
during 2008 over 2007, partially due to $5.1&nbsp;million of higher compensation resulting primarily
from bonuses tied to the successful release of <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>, and increased
employee headcount. SG&#038;A in 2008 also reflects an increase of $0.7&nbsp;million in the advertisement
and promotion costs utilized to increase awareness of the Iron Man character.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">SG&#038;A expenses included in All Other for 2008 decreased $2.1&nbsp;million over 2007, principally
reflecting a decline in the SG&#038;A of our former toy manufacturing activities, which amounted to $0.7
million in 2008 and $9.2&nbsp;million in 2007. This $8.5&nbsp;million decrease was partially offset by a
$4.5&nbsp;million increase in employee compensation expense.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Depreciation and Amortization</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Depreciation and amortization expense decreased $4.4&nbsp;million to $1.6&nbsp;million in 2008 (from
$6.0&nbsp;million in 2007) as a result of decreased tooling costs due to the cessation of our toy
production activities.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We account for our goodwill under the provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;142, &#147;Goodwill and Other
Intangible Assets&#148; (&#147;SFAS 142&#148;). Accordingly, goodwill is not amortized but is subject to annual
impairment tests. Our most recent annual impairment review did not result in an impairment charge.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Other Income</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Other income
increased $20.8&nbsp;million to $23.4&nbsp;million in 2008 (from $2.6&nbsp;million in 2007).
During 2008, we received settlement payments from two interactive licensees in connection with the
early termination of their agreements, which resulted in $19.0&nbsp;million of income, and settlement
payments of $2.0&nbsp;million from a licensee in connection with a contractual violation.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated operating income increased $93.6&nbsp;million to $368.0&nbsp;million during 2008, primarily
reflecting a $119.3&nbsp;million contribution from the gross profit of the Film Production segment
related to the theatrical releases of <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>and the benefits of $21.0
million of licensing settlement payments and decreases in SG&#038;A expenses. These increases were
partially offset by a $50.8&nbsp;million decrease in net sales from the Licensing segment, which
generates the highest margin, which resulted in our consolidated operating margin declining from
56% in 2007 to 54% in 2008.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->27<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Operating income in the Licensing segment decreased $13.2&nbsp;million, primarily because of the
$50.8&nbsp;million reduction in Licensing segment net sales resulting from the decrease in Joint Venture
revenue related to the May&nbsp;2007 release of Spider-Man 3, partially offset by the $21.0&nbsp;million in
licensing settlement payments and the decrease in SG&#038;A discussed above. The operating margin in
the Licensing segment during 2008 increased to 83%, compared with 74% in 2007, primarily resulting
from the licensing settlement payments and the non-recurring SG&#038;A items.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Operating income in the Publishing segment decreased $6.2&nbsp;million, and margins declined from
43% during 2007 to 38% during 2008, primarily due to a $2.9&nbsp;million increase in expenses related to
our digital media initiatives in 2008 compared with 2007. In addition, there was a decrease in
2008 in net sales of custom comic books and advertising, both of which have high margins, combined
with an increase in cost of sales associated with talent costs and paper costs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In 2008, operating
income in the Film Production segment reflects a $119.3&nbsp;million gross
profit contribution from our self-produced films. This was partially offset by $15.7&nbsp;million in
SG&#038;A expenses of the Film Production segment. During 2007, the Film Production operating loss
reflects the SG&#038;A costs noted above and a charge of $0.9&nbsp;million for the decrease in the fair value
of the interest rate cap associated with our film facility, offset by a $2.1&nbsp;million increase in
the fair value of forward contracts for the Canadian dollar. The forward contracts were entered
into during 2007 in connection with the production of <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">All Other operating costs primarily represent corporate overhead expenses, partially offset by
our toy manufacturing operations, which ceased during early 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">From 2007 to 2008, there was an increase in the amount of interest we incurred as a result of
increased outstanding borrowings associated with the <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>productions.
In addition, we capitalized less interest in 2008 than in 2007 because of the completion, early in
2008, of these productions. The result was a $5.2&nbsp;million increase in interest expense in 2008
compared to 2007. We expect that our 2009 interest expense will be lower than in 2008 as our
average outstanding borrowings decrease and we once again begin to capitalize interest.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Interest Income</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Interest income reflects amounts earned on cash equivalents and short-term investments.
Interest income increased $1.0&nbsp;million to $3.6&nbsp;million in 2008 as compared to 2007, due to higher
average cash and investment balances partially offset by a decrease in interest rates.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Gain on Repurchase of Debt</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">During 2008, our subsidiary, MVL International C.V repurchased all $60.0&nbsp;million of our
mezzanine debt related to the film facility for $58.1&nbsp;million, which resulted in a net gain of $1.9
million.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->28<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Income Taxes</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our annual effective tax rate differs from the federal statutory rate due to the effects of
state and local taxes and elimination of the minority share of Joint Venture earnings from reported
income before income tax expense. Our effective tax rates for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008
and 2007 were both 37.6%. While the rates are consistent from year to year, there were offsetting
components. The principal offsetting items were a decrease in state taxes (2.1%) partially offset
by a reduction in the benefit from Joint Venture income (1.7%).
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We are not responsible for the income taxes related to the minority share of the Joint
Venture&#146;s earnings. The tax liability associated with the minority share of the Joint Venture&#146;s
earnings is therefore not reported in our income tax expense, even though the Joint Venture&#146;s
entire income is consolidated in our reported income before income tax expense. Joint Venture
earnings therefore have the effect of lowering our effective tax rate. This effect is more
pronounced in periods in which Joint Venture earnings are higher relative to our other earnings.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We retain various state and local net operating loss carryforwards of $316&nbsp;million, which will
expire in various jurisdictions in the years 2009 through 2026. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, there is
a valuation allowance of $0.3&nbsp;million against capital loss carryforwards, as we believe it is more
likely than not that those assets will not be realized in the future.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We file income tax returns in the U.S. federal jurisdiction and various state, local and
foreign jurisdictions. We are no longer subject to tax examinations by taxing authorities in any
jurisdiction for 2002 and prior tax years. The Internal Revenue Service (&#147;IRS&#148;) is examining our
2003 through 2006 tax years. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, the IRS has proposed certain adjustments,
which are fully reserved for and, if accepted, would not result in a material change to our
financial position. We are also under examination by various state and local jurisdictions.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Minority Interest</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Minority interest related to the Joint Venture amounted to $15.8&nbsp;million in 2008 and $24.5
million in 2007. This $8.7&nbsp;million decrease reflects decreased operations in 2008 from licensing
associated with <I>Spider-Man 3, </I>which was released in May&nbsp;2007.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Earnings per Share</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Diluted earnings per share increased to $2.61 in 2008 from $1.70 in 2007 reflecting a 47%
increase in net income and a 5% reduction in the weighted average number of shares outstanding due
to treasury share repurchases. We repurchased 0.4&nbsp;million shares during 2008.
</DIV>

</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our consolidated net sales of $485.8&nbsp;million for 2007 were $134.0&nbsp;million higher than net
sales in 2006. This was principally due to the160% increase in Licensing segment net sales, which
was mostly attributable to revenue related to <I>Spider-Man 3 </I>merchandising through the joint venture
with Sony Pictures, called Spider-Man Merchandising L.P. (the &#147;Joint Venture&#148;). In addition,
Publishing segment net sales increased 16%. These increases were partially offset by an 85%
decline in sales within All Other due to our exit from toy manufacturing operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Licensing segment net sales increased $211.2&nbsp;million during 2007, mostly due to a $117.9
million increase in Joint Venture revenue related to the May&nbsp;2007 release of <I>Spider-Man 3</I>. There
was no merchandise licensing revenue recorded for <I>Spider-Man 3 </I>until the first quarter of 2007,
when licensees were first permitted to begin exploiting merchandise relating to <I>Spider-Man 3</I>. In
addition, $70.9&nbsp;million of royalty and service fee revenue was recorded in 2007, associated with
Hasbro&#146;s sales to retailers, compared to $5.2&nbsp;million in 2006, which represents the initial sales
under the Hasbro license agreement during the fourth quarter of 2006. Licensing segment net sales
also benefited during 2007 from $18.3&nbsp;million received in settlements of audit claims, an unusually
high amount, and a $4.0&nbsp;million increase in overages revenue. Studio licensing revenue increased
$5.8&nbsp;million, principally due to revenues associated with the Spider-Man movie properties. The
significant increase in Joint Venture revenue and royalty and service revenue from Hasbro caused
2007 Licensing segment net sales to increase as a percentage of consolidated net sales from 38% in
2006 to 71% in 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net sales from the Publishing segment increased $17.2&nbsp;million to $125.7&nbsp;million in 2007,
primarily due to an increase of $10.0&nbsp;million in sales of trade paperbacks and hard cover books and
an increase of $3.9&nbsp;million in comic book sales. The increase in trade paperbacks and hard covers
is attributable to an increase in the sale of <I>Civil War </I>and <I>Dark Tower </I>trade paperbacks. The
growth also reflects an increase in trade titles published. Comic book sales in 2007 benefited
from strong unit sales of the final two comic-book issues of the <I>Civil War </I>series; <I>The Death of
Captain America; </I>the Stephen King series, <I>Dark Tower: The Gunslinger Born; </I>and the <I>World War Hulk</I>
series. Custom publishing also increased $2.3&nbsp;million due to an increase in the scale and number
of projects. Although Publishing segment net sales increased from 2006 to 2007, because of the
larger relative increases in Licensing segment net sales, Publishing segment net sales decreased as
a percentage of consolidated net sales from 31% in 2006 to 26% in 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net sales included in All Other represent our former toy manufacturing operations. Net sales
related to these operations in 2007 were principally sales of licensed-in properties manufactured
by us, and in 2006, were principally sales of Marvel character-based toys manufactured by us.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->30<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<TR style="font-size: 1px">
    <TD><DIV style="margin-left:15px; text-indent:-15px">&nbsp;</DIV></TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
        <TD nowrap colspan="2" align="right" style="border-top: 3px double #000000">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>

    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>

    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
</TR>
<!-- End Table Body --></TABLE>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated cost of revenues decreased $42.7&nbsp;million to $60.9&nbsp;million during 2007 compared
with 2006, primarily due to the reduction of toy-production costs resulting from our cessation of
the manufacture and sale of Marvel-branded toys. Consequently, our consolidated cost of revenues as
a percentage of sales decreased to 13% during 2007, as compared to 29% in 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Publishing segment cost of revenues as a percentage of Publishing segment net sales decreased
from 44% during 2006 to 42% during 2007. Rising costs of talent, ink and paper in 2007 were
absorbed by higher unit sales of comic and trade books. In addition, larger print runs in 2007
resulted in lower unit costs and generated higher margins. The increase in cost of revenue of $5.0
million is primarily associated with increased sales.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Cost of revenues included in All Other primarily consisted of our former toy production
activities. These cost of revenues consisted of product and package manufacturing, shipping and
buying agents&#146; commissions. The most significant portion of
these cost of revenues was product and
package manufacturing. The decrease in these cost of revenues from
$56.4&nbsp;million in 2006 to $8.7&nbsp;million in 2007 reflected the elimination of manufacturing costs to
produce Marvel-branded toys.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated SG&#038;A expenses of $147.1&nbsp;million in 2007 were $24.0&nbsp;million greater than SG&#038;A
expenses in 2006, primarily due to increases in the Licensing segment. Consolidated SG&#038;A as a
percentage of net sales decreased to 30% in 2007 from 35% during 2006, primarily due to the
significant increase in Joint Venture Licensing segment net sales.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->31<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Licensing segment SG&#038;A expenses in 2007 reflected increases of $10.0&nbsp;million in royalties
payable to movie studios, $7.2&nbsp;million in agents&#146; foreign sales commissions and $11.8&nbsp;million in
royalties payable to actors for use of their likeness in licensed products. There were also
increases in connection with licensing promotion expense related to the Joint Venture and increased
professional fees as we pursued audits of our licensees. In addition, during 2007, we recorded a
non-recurring charge of $4.7&nbsp;million related to a contractual obligation. As a percentage of
Licensing segment net sales, Licensing segment SG&#038;A decreased significantly from 38% to 25%. This
resulted from the significant increase in licensing revenue derived from the activities of the
Joint Venture, of which Sony Pictures&#146; share is reflected as minority interest expense rather than
SG&#038;A.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Publishing segment SG&#038;A expenses increased $2.7&nbsp;million during 2007 over 2006, principally due
to increased employee compensation and increased distribution fees associated with increased sales.
Publishing segment SG&#038;A expenses as a percentage of Publishing segment net sales during 2007
remained consistent with the percentage in 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The $2.7&nbsp;million increase in Film Production SG&#038;A expenses in 2007 compared to 2006 reflects
the ramp-up of our film production business, which was partially offset by the capitalization of
overhead costs related to <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>and <I>Iron Man </I>film productions aggregating $1.0
million during 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">SG&#038;A expenses included in All Other for 2007 decreased $18.8&nbsp;million over 2006, principally
reflecting a $18.1&nbsp;million decline in the SG&#038;A of our former toy manufacturing
activities, from
$27.3&nbsp;million in 2006 to $9.2&nbsp;million in 2007. This decrease was principally the result of the
establishment of additional reserves in 2006 for estimated uncollectible amounts due from Toy Biz
Worldwide, Ltd. (&#147;TBW&#148;) in the amount of $2.6&nbsp;million, which were reduced by $1.4&nbsp;million in 2007
upon settling with TBW. This caused a decrease of $4.0&nbsp;million in SG&#038;A in 2007 compared with the
prior year. As a result of the shift to Hasbro , SG&#038;A of our former toy manufacturing activities
also declined due to decreases in general selling expenses of $3.1&nbsp;million, royalties payable to
studios of $3.7&nbsp;million and payroll of $5.3&nbsp;million. Decreases related to corporate overhead were
primarily due to a non-recurring credit in 2007 associated with pension accounting for the
Fleer/Skybox International Retirement Plan and a $0.4&nbsp;million decrease in consulting and payroll
related expenses. This was partially offset by a $1.8&nbsp;million increase in legal fees.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Depreciation and Amortization</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Depreciation and amortization expense decreased $8.3&nbsp;million to $6.0&nbsp;million in 2007 (from
$14.3&nbsp;million in 2006) as a result of decreased tooling costs due to the cessation of our
production of Marvel-branded toys.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We account for our goodwill under the provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;142, &#147;Goodwill and Other
Intangible Assets&#148; (&#147;SFAS 142&#148;). Accordingly, goodwill is not amortized but is subject to annual
impairment tests. Our most recent annual impairment review did not result in an impairment charge.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Other Income</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Other income increased $0.8&nbsp;million to $2.6&nbsp;million in 2007 (from $1.8&nbsp;million in 2006).
During 2007, other income primarily resulted from $2.1&nbsp;million in realized gains generated from
forward contracts entered into during 2007 to mitigate our risk of fluctuations in the Canadian
dollar related to the Canadian filming of <I>The Incredible Hulk. </I>Other income also benefited from
$0.6&nbsp;million in sales of fully depreciated tooling used by our former Toy segment. These increases
were partially offset by a $0.9&nbsp;million decrease in the fair value of the interest rate cap
associated with the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">During 2006, other income consisted principally of $1.6&nbsp;million of non-recurring income
resulting from payments received for our agreement to vacate leased property earlier than provided
for in a lease and our agreement to allow our tenant to vacate property earlier than provided for
in its lease and $0.8&nbsp;million in sales of fully depreciated tooling used by our former Toy segment.
These increases were offset by a $1.5&nbsp;million decrease in the fair value of the interest rate cap
associated with the film facility.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->32<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Consolidated operating income increased $161.8&nbsp;million to $274.4&nbsp;million during 2007,
primarily due to a $211.2&nbsp;million increase in net sales from the Licensing segment, which generates
the highest margins. This increase was partially offset by an 85% decline in sales within All
Other due to our exit from toy manufacturing operations. This also caused consolidated operating
margins to increase significantly, from 32% during 2006 to 56% during 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Operating income in the Licensing segment increased $173.4&nbsp;million, which caused operating
margins to increase from 62% to 74%, primarily due to the increase in merchandise licensing revenue
from the Joint Venture. The margins of Joint Venture merchandise licensing are higher than most
other merchandise licensing because Sony Pictures&#146; share of the Joint Venture&#146;s operating results
is classified as minority interest expense, whereas other studios&#146; shares of license royalty income
is recorded within SG&#038;A expense.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Operating income in the Publishing segment increased $9.4&nbsp;million and margins improved from
41% in 2006 to 43% in 2007 due to the increase in net sales volume with improved gross margins
resulting from larger print runs of more successful titles.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">During 2007, the Film Production operating loss reflects the SG&#038;A costs noted above and a
charge of $0.9&nbsp;million for the decrease in the fair value of the interest rate cap associated with
our film facility, offset by a $2.1&nbsp;million increase in the fair value of forward contracts for the
Canadian dollar. The forward contracts were entered into during 2007 in connection with the
production of <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>. During 2006, the Film Production operating loss reflects the
SG&#038;A costs noted above and a charge of $1.5&nbsp;million for the decrease in the fair value of the
interest rate cap.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">All Other operating costs primarily represent corporate overhead expenses, and the operating
loss of our former toy manufacturing operations of $4.7&nbsp;million in 2007 and the operating income
for these operations of $16.6&nbsp;million in 2006. The $21.0&nbsp;million increase in operating costs is
the result of the $21.3&nbsp;million decrease in the operating income of our former toy manufacturing
operations.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->33<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">From 2006 to 2007, there was a $6.8&nbsp;million increase for interest we incurred. The increase
was primarily the result of borrowings for the movie productions <I>Iron Man </I>(beginning in the first
quarter of 2007) and <I>The Incredible Hulk </I>(beginning in the second quarter of 2007), offset by a
decrease in our short-term borrowings under our HSBC line of credit to finance repurchases of our
common stock. During 2007, $8.4&nbsp;million of interest cost related to movie production borrowings
was capitalized into film inventory, an increase of $8.2&nbsp;million over the 2006 amount. This led to
a $1.4&nbsp;million net reduction in interest expense from 2006 to 2007.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Interest Income</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Interest income reflects amounts earned on our cash equivalents and short-term investments.
Interest income increased $1.1&nbsp;million to $2.6&nbsp;million in 2007 as compared to 2006, due to higher
levels of cash equivalents and short-term investments in 2007 than in 2006.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Income Taxes</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our annual effective tax rate differs from the federal statutory rate due to the effects of
state and local taxes and elimination of the minority share of Joint Venture earnings from reported
income before income tax expense. Our effective tax rates for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2007
and 2006 were 37.6% and 39.5%, respectively. The 1.9% reduction in the effective tax rate was
principally caused by higher Joint Venture income, partially offset by state and local income
taxes.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">As of December&nbsp;31, 2007, we retained various state and local net operating loss carryforwards
of $354&nbsp;million. As of December&nbsp;31, 2007, there was a valuation allowance of $1.2&nbsp;million against
capital loss carryforwards and state and foreign net operating loss carryforwards.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Minority Interest</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Minority interest expense, related to the Joint Venture, amounted to $24.5&nbsp;million in 2007 and
$1.0&nbsp;million in 2006. This increase of $23.5&nbsp;million reflects the increased operations from
licensing associated with the Spider-Man films, the most recent of which was the May&nbsp;2007 release
of <I>Spider-Man 3</I>.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Earnings per Share</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Diluted earnings per share increased to $1.70 in 2007 from $0.67 in 2006 reflecting a 138%
increase in net income and a 5.5% reduction in the weighted average number of shares outstanding
due to the effect of treasury share repurchases (8.5&nbsp;million shares acquired in 2007).
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%"><B>Liquidity and Capital Resources</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our primary sources of liquidity are cash, cash equivalents, cash flows from operations, our
film credit facilities and the HSBC line of credit, described below. We anticipate that our
primary uses for liquidity will be to conduct our business, including our obligation to fund 33% of
the budget of each of our prospective self-produced films commencing with Iron Man 2 and Thor in
2009, and to repurchase our common stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">During 2008, our cash and cash equivalents increased by $75.2&nbsp;million as a result of the
$181.3&nbsp;million of net cash provided by operating activities, which represents an increase of $187.9
million over the $6.6&nbsp;million of cash used in operating activities during 2007. The increase is
principally the result of strong cash collections from our Licensing segment and a $197.9&nbsp;million
reduction in film expenditures in 2008 over 2007. Our film-production expenditures, including
expenditures funded by draw-downs from our film facilities, appear on our accompanying consolidated
statements of cash flows as cash used in operating activities. The related draw-downs appear on
our accompanying consolidated statements of cash flows as cash provided by financing activities.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->34<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our working capital deficiency decreased $82.2&nbsp;million from $108.5&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31,
2007 to $26.3&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31, 2008, primarily reflecting a $115.8&nbsp;million increase in
accounts receivable, which primarily relates to fourth quarter <I>Iron Man </I>revenue that we received
from our distributor in February&nbsp;2009, and the $181.3&nbsp;million of cash generated through operations
noted above. These improvements in working capital were partially offset by the repurchase of $60
million of long-term mezzanine debt using cash from operations and a $162.5&nbsp;million increase in
short-term borrowings principally representing the amount of borrowings under our film facility
that we estimate to be repaid over the twelve-month period beginning on January&nbsp;1, 2009.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net cash flows used in investing activities for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 reflects the
use of restricted cash to fund production expenses and distribute cash to our Joint Venture
partner, and the purchase of short-term investments using cash from operations. Cash equivalents
at December&nbsp;31, 2008 consist principally of U.S. Treasury Bills and short-term bank CD&#146;s (covered
by the U.S. Government&#146;s Temporary Liquidity Guarantee Program). Net cash flows used in investing
activities for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2007 reflect the purchase of short-term investments
using our excess cash and the use of restricted cash to fund production expenses and obligations
under the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Net cash used in financing activities during the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 reflects $180.5
million in repayments of our film facility borrowings, including our repurchase of all $60.0
million of long-term mezzanine debt for $58.1&nbsp;million using cash from operations, partially offset
by $106.3&nbsp;million in borrowings under our film facility during that period. In addition, we
repurchased 0.4&nbsp;million shares of our common stock at a cost of $10.5&nbsp;million. During the year
ended December&nbsp;31, 2007, we repurchased 8.5&nbsp;million shares of our common stock at a cost of $212.0
million. Repurchases were financed through cash generated from operations. At December&nbsp;31, 2008,
the remaining amount authorized and available for stock repurchases was $127.7&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">MVL Film Finance LLC maintains a $525&nbsp;million credit facility for producing theatrical motion
pictures based on our characters. MVL Film Finance LLC&#146;s ability to borrow under the film facility
expires on September&nbsp;1, 2012. The film facility expires on September&nbsp;1, 2016, subject to extension
by up to ten months under certain circumstances. The expiration date and final date for borrowings
under the film facility occur sooner if the films produced under the facility fail to meet certain
defined performance measures. The film facility consists of $465&nbsp;million in revolving senior bank
debt and $60&nbsp;million in mezzanine debt, which is subordinated to the senior bank debt and, as
discussed below, was repurchased by us. Both Standard &#038; Poor&#146;s, a division of the McGraw-Hill
Companies, Inc., and Moody&#146;s Investor Rating Service, Inc. have given the senior bank debt
investment grade ratings. In addition, Ambac Assurance Corporation has insured repayment of the
senior bank debt. In exchange for the repayment insurance, we pay Ambac a fee calculated as a
percentage of senior bank debt, but in no event less than $3.4&nbsp;million per year. The interest
rates for outstanding senior bank debt, and the fees payable on unused senior bank debt capacity,
both described below, include the percentage fee owed to Ambac (assuming the minimum has been
reached). During 2008, our wholly-owned subsidiary, MVL International C.V. repurchased all $60
million of the mezzanine debt for $58.1&nbsp;million. The mezzanine debt remains outstanding and MVL
International C.V. receives the interest payments made by MVL Film Finance LLC with respect to this
debt. The interest expense and interest income related to the mezzanine debt are therefore
eliminated in our consolidated results and our consolidated financial position does not include the
mezzanine debt.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">All future interest payable under the film facility must now be paid from the films&#146; net
collections, rather than from any of our other sources of cash. Effective December&nbsp;31, 2008, the
film facility requires us to maintain a liquidity reserve of $25&nbsp;million, included in restricted
cash, to cover future interest payments in the event that the films&#146; net collections are not
sufficient to make these payments. If we do not release a film in 2010, 2011 and 2012 or our sixth
film under the facility by August&nbsp;26, 2012, the liquidity reserve requirement will be increased to
$45&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The film facility also requires us to maintain an interest reserve equal to the subsequent
quarter&#146;s estimated interest. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, this reserve was $6.4&nbsp;million, and is
included in restricted cash.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In February&nbsp;2009, we repaid $109&nbsp;million of our film facility debt using net collections from
<I>Iron Man</I>.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->35<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The interest rate for the mezzanine debt is LIBOR plus 7.0%. As noted above, we repurchased
the mezzanine debt and, accordingly, interest expense, from the dates of repurchase, related to the
mezzanine debt is not reflected in our consolidated operating results.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The interest rate for outstanding senior bank debt is currently LIBOR (1.43% at December&nbsp;31,
2008) or the commercial paper rate, as applicable, plus 2.935%. The LIBOR rate on our outstanding
senior bank debt resets to the quoted LIBOR rate two business days preceding the commencement of
each calendar quarter. The commercial paper rate resets periodically depending on how often our
lenders issue commercial paper to fund their portion of our outstanding debt. The weighted average
interest rate of our senior bank debt was 5.78% at December&nbsp;31, 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The film facility requires us to pay a fee on any senior bank debt capacity that we are not
using. This fee is currently 0.90%, and is applied on $465&nbsp;million reduced by the amount of any
outstanding senior bank debt.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In June&nbsp;2008, Ambac&#146;s rating was downgraded by S&#038;P from AAA to AA. The downgrade caused an
increase of 1.30% in our interest rate for outstanding senior bank debt and an increase of 0.30% in
the fee payable on our unused senior bank debt capacity. These increases are reflected in the
rates noted above. Any further downgrades of Ambac&#146;s rating, such as the one by Moody&#146;s on
November&nbsp;5, 2008 (to Baa1), do not affect our rate of interest or fees under the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">If the senior bank debt&#146;s rating (without giving effect to Ambac&#146;s insurance) by either S&#038;P or
Moody&#146;s were to fall below investment grade, the interest rate for the outstanding senior bank debt
would increase by up to an additional 0.815%. In addition, if the ratio of our indebtedness,
excluding the film facility, to our total capital, defined as our consolidated equity plus
indebtedness excluding film facility indebtedness, were to exceed 0.4 to 1.0, then the interest
rate for outstanding senior bank debt could increase by up to an additional 0.50%. In light of
recent adverse developments in the credit markets, we have assessed the economic impact on our film
production activities from the actual and potential increases in interest rates described above.
We do not believe the actual or potential impact from these rate increases to be material.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The film facility requires the maintenance of a minimum tangible net worth, a prospective cash
coverage test, an historical cash coverage test and an asset coverage ratio, each measured
quarterly, and compliance with various administrative covenants. We have maintained compliance
with all required provisions of the film facility since its inception.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Until recently, we would also have been required, before our fifth film&#146;s funding, either to
obtain a cumulative, minimum budget percentage (33%) from our pre-sales of film distribution rights
in Australia and New Zealand, Japan, Germany, France and Spain (the &#147;Reserved Territories&#148;),
together with the proceeds of any government rebate, subsidy or tax incentive and any other source
of co-financing, or to fund that budget percentage with our own cash (the &#147;Pre-Sales Test&#148;). The
Pre-Sale Test has now been effectively subsumed by other terms of the film facility, as explained
below. Future distribution in the Reserved Territories will be handled by Paramount, with limited
exceptions.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">The film facility requires us to fund 33% of the budget of each film distributed under the
distribution agreement with Paramount that we entered into in the third quarter of 2008. The film
facility will provide up to 67% of the budget (reduced by the proceeds of any co-financing). After
deduction of Paramount&#146;s distribution fees and expenses in the Reserved Territories, we will be
entitled to recoup our 33% contribution from all film proceeds from the Reserved Territories. Our
recoupment will be crossed among all films distributed under the distribution agreement with
Paramount and among all Reserved Territories. After recoupment of our 33% contribution, all
additional film proceeds from the Reserved Territories will be used to pay down borrowings under
the film facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In January&nbsp;2009, we repaid $16.3 of film facility borrowings with our own cash. That payment,
combined with the pre-sales of distribution rights in the Reserved Territories and other
co-financing proceeds for <I>Iron Man </I>and <I>The Incredible Hulk</I>, equals 33% of those films&#146; budgets.
Because of the requirement that we fund 33% of future films, we therefore expect to be in
compliance, without any further action on our part, with the Pre-Sale Test when it is first applied
(upon funding of the fifth film) and at all relevant times thereafter.
</DIV>
<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">&nbsp;

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->36<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif; margin-left: .25in; width: 7.20in">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In the first quarter of 2009, we amended the film facility to allow us, at our option, to
utilize a lower cost completion bond structure. In order to take advantage of this lower cost
completion bond structure for a picture, we will need to escrow approximately $26&nbsp;million ($31.5
million for <I>Iron Man 2</I>) for the duration of production. Upon completion of the film, the escrowed
funds will be returned to us. However, the amount of escrowed funds returned to us will be reduced
to the extent that the cost of the film exceeds 110% of its budget.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">If one of the banks in our film facility&#146;s lending consortium were to default in making a
required funding and if we were unable to arrange for a replacement bank, the amount available to
us under the film facility would drop by the amount of the defaulting bank&#146;s unused commitment and
our film productions could be disrupted as a result.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We maintain a $100&nbsp;million revolving line of credit with HSBC Bank USA, National Association
(the &#147;HSBC Line of Credit&#148;) with a sub-limit for the issuance of letters of credit. The HSBC Line
of Credit expires on March&nbsp;31, 2010. Borrowings under the HSBC Line of Credit may be used for
working capital and other general corporate purposes and for repurchases of our common stock. The
HSBC Line of Credit contains customary event-of-default provisions and a default provision based on
our market capitalization. We are in compliance with the covenants of the facility, which include
net income, leverage and free cash flow, as of December&nbsp;31, 2008 and continue to be in compliance.
The HSBC Line of Credit is secured by a lien in (a)&nbsp;our accounts receivable, (b)&nbsp;our rights under
our license with Hasbro and (c)&nbsp;all of our treasury stock repurchased by us after November&nbsp;9, 2005.
Borrowings under the HSBC Line of Credit bear interest at HSBC&#146;s prime rate or, at our option, at
LIBOR plus 1.25% per annum. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, we had no borrowings outstanding under the
HSBC Line of Credit.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">Our capital expenditures for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 and 2007 were $2.4&nbsp;million and
$2.7&nbsp;million, respectively. We do not expect to have significant capital expenditures in 2009.
Capital expenditures do not include film costs that we capitalize into film inventory.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">In September&nbsp;2008, we entered into a lease for the rental of sound stages where we intend to
film our next four self-produced films, and for Marvel Studios&#146; new production and corporate office
space, located in Manhattan Beach, California. The lease has an initial term of three years, with
four successive one-year extension options. We anticipate making aggregate rental payments under
this lease of approximately $14&nbsp;million over the three-year period beginning January&nbsp;1, 2009.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; margin-left: 4%">In February&nbsp;2009, we repurchased 0.3&nbsp;million shares of our common stock for $6.5&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 10pt; text-indent: 4%">We believe that our cash and cash equivalents, cash flows from operations, the film
facilities, and the HSBC line of credit will be sufficient for us to conduct our business,
including our obligation to fund 33% of the budget of each of our prospective self-produced films,
and to make repurchases, if any, under our current stock repurchase program.
</DIV>
